<p><br />3 May:  <a href="/wiki/Roman_Rite" title="Roman Rite">Roman Rite</a>, <a href="/wiki/Protestant_Church_in_Germany" title="Protestant Church in Germany">Protestant Church in Germany</a>

</p>
<br />14 November and 30 June: <a href="/wiki/Eastern_Orthodox_Church" title="Eastern Orthodox Church">Eastern Orthodox Church</a> (Translation of relics on 31 June)<br />17 November: Armenian<br />18 November: Coptic</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Saint_symbolism" title="Saint symbolism">Attributes</a></th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/Red_Martyr" class="mw-redirect" title="Red Martyr">Red Martyr</a>, elderly, bearded man, holding a basket of loaves and a <a href="/wiki/Tau_cross" title="Tau cross">Tau cross</a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Patron_saint" title="Patron saint">Patronage</a></th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/Cape_Verde" title="Cape Verde">Cape Verde</a>; <a href="/wiki/Hatter" class="mw-redirect" title="Hatter">Hatters</a>; <a href="/wiki/Pastry_chef" title="Pastry chef">Pastry chefs</a>; <a href="/wiki/San_Felipe_Pueblo,_New_Mexico" title="San Felipe Pueblo, New Mexico">San Felipe Pueblo</a>; <a href="/wiki/Uruguay" title="Uruguay">Uruguay</a></td></tr></tbody></table>
<p><b>Philip the Apostle</b> (<a href="/wiki/Greek_language" title="Greek language">Greek</a>: <span lang="el">Φίλιππος</span>; <a href="/wiki/Aramaic" title="Aramaic">Aramaic</a>: ܦܝܠܝܦܘܣ; <a href="/wiki/Coptic_language" title="Coptic language">Coptic</a>: <span lang="cop">ⲫⲓⲗⲓⲡⲡⲟⲥ</span>, <i>Philippos</i>) was one of the <a href="/wiki/Twelve_Apostles" class="mw-redirect" title="Twelve Apostles">Twelve Apostles</a> of Jesus according to the <a href="/wiki/New_Testament" title="New Testament">New Testament</a>. Later <a href="/wiki/Christian_tradition" title="Christian tradition">Christian traditions</a> describe Philip as the apostle who preached in <a href="/wiki/Greece" title="Greece">Greece</a>, <a href="/wiki/Syria_(region)" title="Syria (region)">Syria</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Asia-Minor" class="mw-redirect" title="Asia-Minor">Asia-Minor</a>.
</p><p>In the <a href="/wiki/Roman_Rite" title="Roman Rite">Roman Rite</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Feast_day" class="mw-redirect" title="Feast day">feast day</a> of Philip, along with that of <a href="/wiki/James_the_Less" title="James the Less">James the Less</a>, was traditionally observed on 1 May, the anniversary of the dedication of the church dedicated to them in Rome (now called the <a href="/wiki/Santi_Apostoli,_Rome" title="Santi Apostoli, Rome">Church of the Twelve Apostles</a>). In the short-lived calendar reform of 1960, it was transferred to 11 May, but since 1969 it has been assigned to 3 May. The <a href="/wiki/Eastern_Orthodox_Church" title="Eastern Orthodox Church">Eastern Orthodox Church</a> celebrates Philip's feast day on 14 November. 
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Philip_the_Apostle._Detail_of_the_mosaic_in_the_Basilica_of_San_Vitale._Ravena,_Italy.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c7/Philip_the_Apostle._Detail_of_the_mosaic_in_the_Basilica_of_San_Vitale._Ravena%2C_Italy.jpg/220px-Philip_the_Apostle._Detail_of_the_mosaic_in_the_Basilica_of_San_Vitale._Ravena%2C_Italy.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="170" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c7/Philip_the_Apostle._Detail_of_the_mosaic_in_the_Basilica_of_San_Vitale._Ravena%2C_Italy.jpg/330px-Philip_the_Apostle._Detail_of_the_mosaic_in_the_Basilica_of_San_Vitale._Ravena%2C_Italy.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c7/Philip_the_Apostle._Detail_of_the_mosaic_in_the_Basilica_of_San_Vitale._Ravena%2C_Italy.jpg/440px-Philip_the_Apostle._Detail_of_the_mosaic_in_the_Basilica_of_San_Vitale._Ravena%2C_Italy.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1366" data-file-height="1054" /></a><figcaption>Philip the Apostle, detail of the mosaic in the <a href="/wiki/Basilica_of_San_Vitale" title="Basilica of San Vitale">Basilica of San Vitale</a>, <a href="/wiki/Ravenna" title="Ravenna">Ravenna</a>, 6th century</figcaption></figure>
<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" />
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="New_Testament">New Testament</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Philip_the_Apostle&amp;action=edit&amp;section=1" title="Edit section: New Testament">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Synoptic_Gospels" title="Synoptic Gospels">Synoptic Gospels</a> list Philip as one of the apostles. The <a href="/wiki/Gospel_of_John" title="Gospel of John">Gospel of John</a> recounts Philip's calling as a <a href="/wiki/Disciple_(Christianity)" title="Disciple (Christianity)">disciple</a> of Jesus.<sup id="cite_ref-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-1">&#91;1&#93;</a></sup> Philip is described as a disciple from the city of <a href="/wiki/Bethsaida" title="Bethsaida">Bethsaida</a>, and the evangelist connects him with <a href="/wiki/Saint_Andrew" class="mw-redirect" title="Saint Andrew">Andrew</a> and <a href="/wiki/Saint_Peter" title="Saint Peter">Peter</a>, who were from the same town. He also was among those surrounding John the Baptist when the latter first pointed out Jesus as the <a href="/wiki/Lamb_of_God" title="Lamb of God">Lamb of God</a>. It was Philip who first introduced <a href="/wiki/Nathanael_(follower_of_Jesus)" title="Nathanael (follower of Jesus)">Nathanael</a> (sometimes identified with <a href="/wiki/Bartholomew_the_Apostle" title="Bartholomew the Apostle">Bartholomew</a>) to Jesus.<sup id="cite_ref-Kirsch_2-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Kirsch-2">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup> According to <a href="/wiki/Alban_Butler" title="Alban Butler">Butler</a>, Philip was among those attending the wedding at Cana.<sup id="cite_ref-Butler_3-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Butler-3">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Of the four Gospels, Philip figures most prominently in the Gospel of John.<sup id="cite_ref-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-4">&#91;a&#93;</a></sup> Jesus tests Philip (John 6:6) when he asks him how to feed the 5,000 people.<sup id="cite_ref-Kirsch_2-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Kirsch-2">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup> Later he appears as a link to the <a href="/wiki/Greeks" title="Greeks">Greek</a> community. Philip bore a <a href="/wiki/Greek_name" title="Greek name">Greek name</a>, could likely speak Greek,<sup id="cite_ref-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-5">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup> and may have been known to the Greek pilgrims in Jerusalem. He advises Andrew that certain Greeks wish to meet Jesus, and together they inform Jesus of this (John 12:21).<sup id="cite_ref-Kirsch_2-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Kirsch-2">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup> During the <a href="/wiki/Last_Supper" title="Last Supper">Last Supper</a>, when Philip asked Jesus to show them the Father, he provides Jesus the opportunity to teach his disciples about the unity of the <a href="/wiki/God_the_Father" title="God the Father">Father</a> and the <a href="/wiki/God_the_Son" title="God the Son">Son</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Butler_3-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Butler-3">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Distinct_from_Philip_the_Evangelist">Distinct from Philip the Evangelist</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Philip_the_Apostle&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: Distinct from Philip the Evangelist">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Philip the Apostle should not be confused with <a href="/wiki/Philip_the_Evangelist" title="Philip the Evangelist">Philip the Evangelist</a>, who was appointed with <a href="/wiki/Saint_Stephen" title="Saint Stephen">Stephen</a>  and five others to oversee charitable distributions (Acts 6:5).<sup id="cite_ref-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-6">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Apocryphal_accounts">Apocryphal accounts</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Philip_the_Apostle&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: Apocryphal accounts">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Nag_Hammadi_texts">Nag Hammadi texts</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Philip_the_Apostle&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: Nag Hammadi texts">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>One of the <a href="/wiki/Gnostic" class="mw-redirect" title="Gnostic">Gnostic</a> <a href="/wiki/Codices" class="mw-redirect" title="Codices">codices</a> discovered in the <a href="/wiki/Nag_Hammadi_library" title="Nag Hammadi library">Nag Hammadi library</a> in 1945 bears Philip's name in its title, on the bottom line.<sup id="cite_ref-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-7">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup>
An early extra-biblical story about St. Philip is preserved in the apocryphal <i><a href="/wiki/Letter_of_Peter_to_Philip" title="Letter of Peter to Philip">Letter from Peter to Philip</a></i>, also one of the texts in the <a href="/wiki/Nag_Hammadi_Library" class="mw-redirect" title="Nag Hammadi Library">Nag Hammadi Library</a>, and dated to the end of the 2nd century or early 3rd.<sup id="cite_ref-8" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-8">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup> This text begins with a letter from St. Peter to St. Philip, asking him to rejoin the other apostles who had gathered at the <a href="/wiki/Mount_of_Olives" title="Mount of Olives">Mount of Olives</a>. Fred Lapham believes that this letter indicates an early tradition that "at some point between the Resurrection of Jesus and the final parting of his risen presence from the disciples, Philip had undertaken a sole missionary enterprise, and was, for some reason, reluctant to return to the rest of the Apostles." This mission is in harmony with the later tradition that each disciple was given a specific missionary charge.<sup id="cite_ref-9" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-9">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup> Lapham explains the central section, a <a href="/wiki/Gnosticism" title="Gnosticism">Gnostic</a> dialogue between the risen Christ and his disciples, as a later insertion.<sup id="cite_ref-10" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-10">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup> 
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Acts:_miracles_and_martyrdom"><i>Acts</i>: miracles and martyrdom</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Philip_the_Apostle&amp;action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: Acts: miracles and martyrdom">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Later stories about Philip's life can be found in the anonymous <i><a href="/wiki/Acts_of_Philip" title="Acts of Philip">Acts of Philip</a></i>, probably written by a contemporary of Eusebius.<sup id="cite_ref-11" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-11">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup> This non-canonical book recounts the preaching and miracles of Philip. According to these accounts, following the resurrection of Jesus, Philip was sent with his sister <a href="/w/index.php?title=Mariamne_(sister_of_the_Apostle_Philip)&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Mariamne (sister of the Apostle Philip) (page does not exist)">Mariamne</a> and <a href="/wiki/Bartholomew" class="mw-redirect" title="Bartholomew">Bartholomew</a> to preach in <a href="/wiki/Greece" title="Greece">Greece</a>, <a href="/wiki/Phrygia" title="Phrygia">Phrygia</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Syria" title="Syria">Syria</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-12" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-12">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup> Included in the <i>Acts of Philip</i> is an appendix, entitled "Of the Journey of Philip the Apostle: From the Fifteenth Act Until the End, and Among Them the Martyrdom." This appendix gives an embellished account of Philip's martyrdom in the city of <a href="/wiki/Hierapolis" title="Hierapolis">Hierapolis</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-13" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-13">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup> According to this account, through a miraculous healing and his preaching Philip converted the wife of the proconsul of the city. This enraged the proconsul, and he had Philip, Bartholomew, and Mariamne all tortured. Philip and Bartholomew were then <a href="/wiki/Cross_of_St._Peter" class="mw-redirect" title="Cross of St. Peter">crucified upside-down</a>, and Philip preached from his cross. As a result of Philip's preaching the crowd released Bartholomew from his cross, but Philip insisted that they not release him, and Philip died on the cross. Philip is also said to have been martyred by beheading, rather than crucifixion, in the city of <a href="/wiki/Hierapolis" title="Hierapolis">Hierapolis</a>.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Menologion_of_Basil_002.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f8/Menologion_of_Basil_002.jpg/220px-Menologion_of_Basil_002.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="146" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f8/Menologion_of_Basil_002.jpg/330px-Menologion_of_Basil_002.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f8/Menologion_of_Basil_002.jpg/440px-Menologion_of_Basil_002.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1949" data-file-height="1289" /></a><figcaption>Martyrdom of Philip the Apostle. Scene from the <a href="/wiki/Menologion_of_Basil_II" title="Menologion of Basil II">Menologion of Basil II</a>.</figcaption></figure>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Relics">Relics</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Philip_the_Apostle&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: Relics">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>The relics of Philip the Apostle are currently found in the <a href="/wiki/Crypt" title="Crypt">crypt</a> of Basilica <a href="/wiki/Santi_Apostoli,_Rome" title="Santi Apostoli, Rome">Santi Apostoli, Rome</a>,<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> as well as the Church of St. Philip the Apostle in <a href="/wiki/Cheektowaga_(town),_New_York" class="mw-redirect" title="Cheektowaga (town), New York">Cheektowaga, New York</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-14" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-14">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Possible_tomb_location">Possible tomb location</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Philip_the_Apostle&amp;action=edit&amp;section=7" title="Edit section: Possible tomb location">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Tomb_of_Philip_the_Apostle_Hierapolis.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/28/Tomb_of_Philip_the_Apostle_Hierapolis.jpg/220px-Tomb_of_Philip_the_Apostle_Hierapolis.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="146" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/28/Tomb_of_Philip_the_Apostle_Hierapolis.jpg/330px-Tomb_of_Philip_the_Apostle_Hierapolis.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/28/Tomb_of_Philip_the_Apostle_Hierapolis.jpg/440px-Tomb_of_Philip_the_Apostle_Hierapolis.jpg 2x" data-file-width="5148" data-file-height="3428" /></a><figcaption>Tomb of Philip the Apostle, Hierapolis</figcaption></figure>
<p>In 2011, Italian archaeologist Francesco D'Andria claimed to have discovered the original tomb of Philip during excavations in ancient <a href="/wiki/Hierapolis" title="Hierapolis">Hierapolis</a>, close to the modern Turkish city of <a href="/wiki/Denizli" title="Denizli">Denizli</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-BAS_15-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-BAS-15">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup> The 1st-century tomb, found to be empty of relics, stood at the centre of the 4th- or 5th-century<sup id="cite_ref-BAS_15-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-BAS-15">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup> three-naved basilica, the Church of the Sepulchre, which was one of the focal points of an entire ancient pilgrimage hill complex dedicated to Philip. Ancient Greek prayers are carved into the walls of the tomb and church venerating Philip the Apostle, and a 6th-century bread stamp (<i><a href="/w/index.php?title=Sello_de_pan&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Sello de pan (page does not exist)">signum pistoris</a><span class="noprint" style="font-size:85%; font-style: normal;">&#160;&#91;<a href="https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sello_de_pan" class="extiw" title="es:Sello de pan">es</a>&#93;</span></i>) shows Philip holding bread (John 6) with this specific three-naved church on his left side, and the previously identified nearby <a href="/wiki/Martyrion" class="mw-redirect" title="Martyrion">martyrion</a> church to his right, removing all doubts about the basilica being the one to  contain the original tomb of the apostle. The church built on his tomb and the martyrion church some 40 yards away were places of intense veneration for centuries: In Philip's Church of the Sepulchre the marble floors were worn down by thousands of people.<sup id="cite_ref-BAS_15-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-BAS-15">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-16" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-16">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 2012, <a href="/wiki/Bartholomew_I_of_Constantinople" title="Bartholomew I of Constantinople">Bartholomew</a>, the patriarch of Constantinople and primate of the Orthodox church, celebrated the liturgy of St. Philip in the Church of the Sepulchre and in the martyrion church of the apostle.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Iconography">Iconography</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Philip_the_Apostle&amp;action=edit&amp;section=8" title="Edit section: Iconography">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Coa_Illustration_Cross_of_St_Philip.svg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/79/Coa_Illustration_Cross_of_St_Philip.svg/220px-Coa_Illustration_Cross_of_St_Philip.svg.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="170" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/79/Coa_Illustration_Cross_of_St_Philip.svg/330px-Coa_Illustration_Cross_of_St_Philip.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/79/Coa_Illustration_Cross_of_St_Philip.svg/440px-Coa_Illustration_Cross_of_St_Philip.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="388" data-file-height="300" /></a><figcaption>Cross of Philip</figcaption></figure>
<p>Philip is commonly associated with the symbol of the <a href="/wiki/Latin_cross" title="Latin cross">Latin cross</a>. Other symbols assigned to Philip include: the cross with the two loaves (because of his answer to the Lord in John 6:7), a basket filled with bread, a spear with the <a href="/wiki/Patriarchal_cross" title="Patriarchal cross">patriarchal cross</a>, and a cross with a <a href="/wiki/Carpenter%27s_square" class="mw-redirect" title="Carpenter&#39;s square">carpenter's square</a>.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Philippus_San_Giovanni_in_Laterano_2006-09-07.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d6/Philippus_San_Giovanni_in_Laterano_2006-09-07.jpg/220px-Philippus_San_Giovanni_in_Laterano_2006-09-07.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="333" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d6/Philippus_San_Giovanni_in_Laterano_2006-09-07.jpg/330px-Philippus_San_Giovanni_in_Laterano_2006-09-07.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d6/Philippus_San_Giovanni_in_Laterano_2006-09-07.jpg/440px-Philippus_San_Giovanni_in_Laterano_2006-09-07.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1300" data-file-height="1969" /></a><figcaption>Statue of Philip in the <a href="/wiki/Archbasilica_of_St._John_Lateran" class="mw-redirect" title="Archbasilica of St. John Lateran">Archbasilica of St. John Lateran</a> by <a href="/wiki/Giuseppe_Mazzuoli_(1644%E2%80%931725)" title="Giuseppe Mazzuoli (1644–1725)">Giuseppe Mazzuoli</a></figcaption></figure>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Veneration">Veneration</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Philip_the_Apostle&amp;action=edit&amp;section=9" title="Edit section: Veneration">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>Philip is <a href="/wiki/Calendar_of_saints_(Church_of_England)" title="Calendar of saints (Church of England)">remembered</a> (with <a href="/wiki/James,_brother_of_Jesus" title="James, brother of Jesus">James</a>) in the <a href="/wiki/Church_of_England" title="Church of England">Church of England</a> with a <a href="/wiki/Festival_(Anglicanism)" title="Festival (Anglicanism)">Festival</a> on <a href="/wiki/May_1" title="May 1">1 May</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-17" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-17">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Holy and All-praised Apostle Philip is <a href="/wiki/Calendar_of_saints" title="Calendar of saints">commemorated</a> on 14 November and 30 June (<a href="/wiki/Synaxis" title="Synaxis">Synaxis</a> of <a href="/wiki/12_apostles" class="mw-redirect" title="12 apostles">the Holy, Glorious and All-Praised Twelve Apostles</a>) in the <a href="/wiki/Eastern_Orthodox_Church" title="Eastern Orthodox Church">Eastern Orthodox Church</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-18" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-18">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-19" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-19">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>His feast day begins <a href="/wiki/Nativity_Fast" title="Nativity Fast">the Nativity Fast</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Eastern_Orthodox_Church" title="Eastern Orthodox Church">Eastern Orthodox Church</a>, that is called <a href="/wiki/Philip%27s_Fast" class="mw-redirect" title="Philip&#39;s Fast">Philip's Fast</a> (or the <a href="/wiki/Philippian_fast" class="mw-redirect" title="Philippian fast">Philippian Fast</a>),<sup id="cite_ref-20" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-20">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Nativity_Fast" title="Nativity Fast">the Fast</a> is <a href="/wiki/Eastern_Orthodox_Church" title="Eastern Orthodox Church">Eastern</a> equivalent of <a href="/wiki/Western_Church" class="mw-redirect" title="Western Church">Western</a> <a href="/wiki/Advent" title="Advent">Advent</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-21" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-21">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Patronage">Patronage</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Philip_the_Apostle&amp;action=edit&amp;section=10" title="Edit section: Patronage">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>Saint Philip is the patron saint of hatters.<sup id="cite_ref-22" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-22">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
